### 12.3 Schema and composition

```
  versions; bindings { object_hash, interface_hash, closure_hash (required), reference_program_hash, dialect_mapping_version, target_hash, schedule_hash?, invocation? (K13) }
  producer { name, version, materialisation { llvm_version, pipeline, normaliser_version } }
  regions { <closure_hash>: { kind, signature, bounds, estimate? (K12), frame (required typed-record descriptor, §9.4), resources { name: decimal }, gates_used { gate: { count, scheme?, epsilon? } },
            claims: [ <claim> … ], verification: { oracle: i, borrow_frames: [i], inverse_pairs: [ { g, ginv, claim: i, budget: [B,heap,stack,depth], arg_tokens?: { "<arg>": [B,heap,stack,depth] } } ], unitary: [i], channel: [i],
                                                   fault_rate: { domain, faults_by_code, undefined_excluded, layout_dependent_excluded } },
            cleanup: { "<variant>:<inherited_controls>": [ { path, realisation } ] },
            error:   { "<variant>:<inherited_controls>": { local: { "operator_norm"?: v, "diamond"?: v }, inclusive: { "operator_norm"?: v, "diamond"?: v } } },
            definedness_assumptions: [ids] } }
  composition { multiplicities: { "<hash>:<variant>:<inherited_controls>": n (from the realised schedule) }, epsilon_total: { "diamond": v }, method: "sum_over_multiplicities" }
```

**Invocation binding (K13).** An invocation is
`{ args: [value, …], budget: [B,heap,stack,depth] }`, with every value
resolved before replay and all four budget components explicit even
when zero. `args` is in signature order: `iN` and `ptr` values are
canonical **unsigned decimal strings** of exactly that bit-vector
value (ptr has `ptr_width` bits); `f64` is `0x` followed by exactly 16
lowercase hex digits of its IEEE 754 binary64 bit pattern, most
significant nibble first (so −0 and NaN payloads are not lost); a token
is its four-component tuple of canonical nonnegative decimal strings,
not a process-local token id. Budget components are also canonical
nonnegative decimal strings. Signature types disambiguate encodings;
widths/ranges and every tuple MUST pass §5.4 validation. `params_hash`
is SHA-256 of the UTF-8 JCS **args array**, no trailing LF (§9.2), not
of the whole invocation. Thus equal token tuples have equal parameter
hashes regardless of ids, while +0 and −0 `f64` parameters differ.

Each standalone table/program/structural claim MUST carry its own
`invocation`; it binds
**every** classical argument and budget. Missing values have no implicit
zero/default. Claims with no arguments still bind `args: []` and their
budget. K4 inverse-pair claims instead bind the entry's budget,
`arg_tokens` and quantified `args` domain as already specified; this
rule does not replace that quantification by one chosen argument.
A parameter-uniform structural claim MAY instead bind
`invocation: { args: "all", budget: "all" }`: its checker MUST discharge
the rule's side conditions for every well-typed argument vector and
valid budget, not by replaying a chosen instance. Thus generic library
and adjoint/translation proofs retain their universal scope.
A scheduled certificate MUST carry the root `bindings.invocation`;
its args hash and budget MUST equal the root schedule's bindings, and
all nested arguments and budgets MUST be resolved at each realised
invocation context (§12.1), including default-covered contexts.
Explicit nested bindings, if supplied, MUST equal those resolutions;
a root binding MUST NOT be silently reused for a different callee.
A checker MUST reject absent or conflicting bindings before replay,
and an executor may use a verified result only for the invocation(s)
it actually covers. Reference table/program/structural claims need no
schedule; they prove only their reference relation, not a target
realisation. Scheduled/numerical/channel claims retain the expansion
library gate and all existing context and composition obligations.

**CLAIM-BINDING witness**

```json
{"signature":["i128","f64","token"],"args":["18446744073709551617","0x8000000000000000",["2","0","0","0"]],"params_hash":"9f628a694fa7259bf899bfb677aabce1c084e268a738a40f7f643eb6d8c9c093"}
```

The first argument is > 2^64; the second is binary64 negative zero;
the third is a token tuple, not an id. Replacing negative zero with
`0x0000000000000000` changes `params_hash`; replacing the token by
another valid id of the same tuple does not.

`versions` is the object `{ "isa", "object", "launch", "sched", "cert",
"coverage", "target" }` of the seven header versions. A **variant** is a
nonempty string chosen by the producer (the ISA enumerates none); it is
compared bytewise.

`inclusive(R, key) = local(R, key) + Σ_callees n_c × inclusive(c, key_c)`
**per metric**, keyed like `cleanup` by `<variant>:<inherited_controls>`
(the realised error depends on both); every `local` value is a verified
common upper bound over all invocation contexts of its key in the bound
root schedule (§12.1), so multiplying by n_c is sound; an
`operator_norm` value converts
to `diamond` by ×2 once, at the point it is first combined with a
`diamond` value; `operator_norm` conclusions (T6, controlled unitary
variants) require `operator_norm` bounds throughout. A controlled
unitary variant requires a `numerical` claim; an ε > 0 realisation
compared as a channel requires a `channel` claim. Executable claims
(`numerical`, `channel`, and `exhaustive` or `sampled` with
`expected: "oracle"`, plus table/program comparisons of a realised
operation) bind to `schedule_hash` through their subject. Standalone
reference relations have only the invocation scope defined above (K13).

### 9.2 Identity

An lproc's token, classical and region parameter groups are part of
its JCS signature (§9.1), including their order and types. The header
and every forwarded operand list are canonical function bytes; changing
them changes the envelope and, through references, the caller's closure
hash. Fixed binding instances are derived per oracle (§3.7), not new
symbols or separately hashed bodies; instance rows share the source
row's program-point id and expand once per fixed row instance per step,
while the instance and semantic-call occurrence components in §11
invocation paths and §12.1 context keys distinguish their uses. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-12.1-P11] Entry argument values and token tuples bind invocations through
`params_hash`, not through the lproc's interface hash (this section).
Imports must match all five groups; validation and instance formation
are repeated after linking (§9.3).

**Canonical function bytes**: the text from the function's header line
through its last instruction line, lines joined by LF, no trailing LF.
**Semantic envelope** of a symbol (byte-exact; every part ends with LF):

```
  (1) "isa=" <full version string, e.g. 0.9>
  (2) "ptr_width=" INT
  (3) the JCS signature
  (4) the canonical function bytes (with reference tokens replaced, below); for a data symbol: its data line NAME SP HEX
  (5a) for every data entry in the closure set, sorted by name bytewise:        "data " NAME " " HEX
  (5b) for every layout leaf in the closure set, sorted by JCS path bytes: "layout " <JCS key-component array> " " <JCS value>. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
  (5c) for an R/CC function, for every device model and service it uses, sorted: "device " <name> " " <JCS entry>
  (6) for every referenced symbol outside the symbol's SCC, in order of first reference: its 64-hex hash
```

**Closure set**, by symbol kind. **Oracle**: **all** `layout` leaves of
the object except `interface_regions` entries of other oracles (every
global's pre-entry affects its allocation faults), its own `interface_
regions.<oracle>.*` leaves, and every data image those leaves name.
**Lproc**: the leaves and images of every global `@g` it names in `lift.
addr`. **Action / permutation action**: no layout leaves; the `@image`
of each `qregion` is an ordinary symbol reference (replaced in part (4),
listed in part (6)). **CC / R function**: all `layout` leaves, every
data image they name, and in (5c) every device model and `hostcall`
service it uses. **Data symbol**: parts (5)–(6) are empty. A layout leaf
is a scalar or an object at the deepest level the schema lists (`globals.
<name>` and `interface_regions.<oracle>.<region>` are leaves; `ptr_
width`, `H`, … are leaves); a scalar's JCS value is its decimal text.
**Globals are data symbols**: every global `g` in `layout.globals` MUST
have `image` = `g` and the object MUST define a `data` symbol named `g`
of that size, so the token `@g` in `lift.addr` is both the global and a
symbol reference (replaced by the data symbol's hash; the global's
`base` and `size` enter through the layout leaf). An `@name` naming an
interface region (in `lift.addr`) is not a symbol reference and is left
unchanged. **Reference replacement**: in parts
(4)–(5) every `@name` symbol-reference token is replaced by `@` followed
by the referenced symbol's 64-hex hash (the symbol's own name in its
header line is a definition, not a reference, and is left unchanged); the **interface hash** uses
interface hashes of defined symbols and `SHA-256(name ‖ 0x00 ‖ JCS
signature)` (hex) for unresolved imports; the **closure hash** uses
closure hashes. **Acyclic and cyclic symbols.** The reference graph
has an edge from a symbol to every symbol it references (parts
(4)–(5)). An **acyclic** symbol — a one-member SCC that does not
reference itself — has hash = SHA-256 of its own envelope (example 1's
`inc8`, example 2's `zero` and `sum_db`); the SCC procedure below applies
to every **cyclic** SCC, including a one-member SCC whose function
references itself (example 2's `sum`). **SCCs**: members ordered by name bytewise; every intra-
SCC `@name` token (parts (4)–(5)) is replaced by `@$<decimal index>`;
intra-SCC references contribute no part-(6) line; `scc_hash` = SHA-256
over the concatenation of the members' envelopes in that order; member
hash = SHA-256 over the ASCII bytes of `<scc_hash hex>:<decimal index>`.
**object_hash** = SHA-256 of the canonical object text with the
`schedule`, `certificate` and `coverage` sections deleted together with
the blank line preceding each. `reference_program_hash` = the oracle's
interface hash. `params_hash` = SHA-256 of the JCS array of classical
parameter values (the typed encoding of §12.3, K13); `schedule_hash` =
SHA-256 of the schedule's JCS bytes.
**Program-point id** = `(symbol interface hash, block name, instruction

    12	The earlier note correctly identifies that 0.9 §8.3's

    20	
    21	- The extension is nested under `imports.<name>.model`; the old numeric
    22	  import shape is retained. No new top-level `model_parameters` namespace
    23	  or implicit vendor-name binding is needed.
    24	- Source identity hashes **raw supplied bytes**, with the bytes embedded;
    25	  a second hash identifies the canonical normalized model. Canonicalizing
    26	  a vendor file first could lose significant numeric spelling or metadata.
    27	- Units have dimensions and scales, not just labels. Parameters distinguish
    28	  hardware, code distance and algorithm inputs. A code distance is positive

    30	  rule would wrongly exclude codes with even distance.

    33	  risk a gate epsilon. Legacy count charges keep their old meaning.
    34	- Every numeric substitution has a JSON Pointer, unit conversion, rounding
    35	  rule and checked resolved value. The hierarchy has an exact node grammar,
    36	  half-open intervals and single-accounted shared-factory reservations.
    37	- Symbolic models are valid inputs. Unresolved **operational** bindings
    38	  prevent producing a concrete schedule, not loading/executing an otherwise
    39	  valid object. Unsupported source expressions stay losslessly present as
    40	  explicit obligations. That preservation is not a claim of full adapter
    41	  implementation: fd0 remains a blocker until promised adapters work.
    42	
    43	North Star §§4.3–4.4, §5.1 and §7's Qualtran/Azure RE row require all named
    44	resources, independent checking, symbolic hierarchy and models as data.
    45	All §8 demo rungs, especially Grover over unmodified SQLite with its
    46	N=2^20 estimate, remain unchanged. A scalar-only subset, source rewrite,
    47	mandatory gate materialization, or rejecting algorithms because a model
    48	is symbolic would narrow that scope. The proposal below retains symbolic
    49	models and a concrete instantiation path without making either executor
    50	understand a vendor's implementation language.

    62	
    63	### 8.5 Imported models, parameters and hierarchical estimates (proposed normative text)
    64	
    65	An import MAY retain the legacy form `{version:string,map:object}`. Such
    66	an entry has its existing scalar import meaning; no QEC selection,
    67	hierarchy, physical-error guarantee or new parameter is inferred from it.
    68	An entry using this section has exactly `{version,map,model}`. `version`
    69	is the nonempty imported-model version, not the ISA sub-version.
    70	`model.schema` MUST equal `"bennett.import-model/1"`. All fields shown
    71	below are required unless suffixed `?`; absent optional arrays/maps are
    72	empty. Duplicate JSON keys, unknown fields, and unknown schema versions
    73	are invalid model encodings, rather than instructions to ignore data.
    74	
    75	**Scalar and unit types.** In the following schemas `S` is a nonempty
    76	Unicode string compared bytewise, `H` is exactly 64 lowercase hexadecimal
    77	digits, `Hex` is an even-length lowercase hexadecimal string (possibly
    78	empty), and `Q` is a reduced rational encoded as a string: `0` or a
    79	canonical signed decimal numerator, optionally followed by `/` and a
    80	positive canonical denominator greater than one. No leading `+`, leading
    81	zero, `-0`, denominator one, or reducible fraction is permitted. `Z` is
    82	the integral subset of Q, `N` the nonnegative integers, and `P` the strictly
    83	positive rationals. Mathematical integer ranges are unbounded; an implementation
    84	resource limit is an explicit unfinished-operation diagnostic, not a
    85	model-domain restriction or an executed program fault.
    86	
    87	A `Unit` is `{dimension:{S:Q},scale:P}`. Its dimension map omits zero
    88	exponents. Axis `s` denotes seconds; `physical_qubit` and `logical_qubit`
    89	are distinct axes. Other axes MUST include their source format and version in the axis
    90	identifier, unless an explicit dimension-preserving conversion is present in the normalized
    91	formula. An axis spelling itself is its identity; there is no implicit
    92	unit-equivalence registry.
    93	Equal labels alone imply no equivalence. A numeric value x in this unit
    94	has base value x*scale and the given dimension vector. Fractional
    95	exponents are admitted. Dimensionless units have `{}`. Unit metadata is
    96	not an approximation or an instruction to sum quantities.
    97	
    98	A target resource participating in a typed map MUST declare
    99	`dimension:{S:Q}` and `scale:P` in addition to its §8.2 `unit:string`.
   100	Its `unit` remains the display label. These two fields are optional and
   101	have no effect for resources not participating in this section. The
   102	mandatory `time` dimension is `{s:1}`, scale 1. Ordinary logical peak is
   103	`{logical_qubit:1}` and logical occupancy is
   104	`{logical_qubit:1,s:1}`; physical model outputs retain physical axes.
   105	A resource of kind `model` MUST supply this metadata; it has no automatic
   106	§8.2 scheduled-event aggregation and is reported in `model_estimates`.
   107	The required scheduled `time`, `peak_qubits` and `qubit_second` accounting
   108	remains in force. An objective that references a model resource MUST supply an explicit
   109	selector in the new optional `objective.model_outputs` map:
   110	
   111	```text
   112	objective = {weighted:{S:number},model_outputs?:{S:Selector}}
   113	          | {lexicographic:[S,...],model_outputs?:{S:Selector}}
   114	Selector = {model:S,node:S,metric:"duration"|"counts"|"occupancy"|"peak"|"quantity",
   115	            pool?:S,resource?:S,quantity?:S}
   116	```
   117	
   118	Each model-resource objective term has exactly one selector keyed by that
   119	resource name; other selector keys are invalid. Model/node name the
   120	hierarchy to evaluate under its resolved target and current algorithm
   121	bindings. Duration has seconds units and no optional selector field;
   122	counts requires resource, quantity requires its local quantity name, and
   123	occupancy/peak require pool; other optional selector fields are forbidden.
   124	Quantity selects that model output and converts through its map, counts
   125	selects its aggregate count, and pool metrics
   126	select the model-qualified pool. The selected dimensions must agree with
   127	the objective resource's and the value is converted to its scale. Existing
   128	weighted/lexicographic rules then apply to that scalar. An unbound
   129	objective is retained symbolically and has no silently assigned scalar
   130	value or completed concrete optimization verdict.
   131	
   132	**Normalized model schema.** The import key supplies the model name.
   133	Names of units, parameters, quantities and nodes are local to that model.
   134	`model_hash` is SHA-256 of UTF-8 JCS bytes of the complete `model` object,
   135	without a final LF; `source.sha256` below hashes the decoded raw bytes.
   136	
   137	```text
   138	model = {
   139	  schema: "bennett.import-model/1",
   140	  source: {format:S,version:S,media_type:S,bytes:Hex,sha256:H},
   141	  units: {S:Unit},
   142	  parameters: {S:Parameter},
   143	  quantities: {S:Quantity},
   144	  bindings: [Binding,...],
   145	  nodes: {S:Node},
   146	  assumptions: [S,...]
   147	}
   148	Parameter = {
   149	  role: "hardware" | "code_distance" | "algorithm" | "other",
   150	  type: "integer" | "real", unit:S, value: ClosedExpr | null,
   151	  domain: {min:Q|null,max:Q|null,parity:"any"|"odd"|"even"}
   152	}
   153	Quantity = {unit:S,role:"duration"|"demand"|"risk"|"count"|"scalar",expr:Expr}
   154	Binding = {path:S,quantity:S,factor:P,rounding:"exact"|"upper"|"lower",places:N,value:Q|null}
   155	Expr = Q | {lit:Q,unit:S} | {param:S} | {quantity:S}
   156	     | {op:Op,args:[Expr,...]}
   157	     | {external:{location:S,unit:S}}
   158	Op = "+" | "-" | "*" | "/" | "min" | "max" | "ceil" | "pow" | "sqrt"
   159	ClosedExpr = Expr with no param, quantity or external reference
   160	```
   161	
   162	Every unit reference MUST resolve in `units`. `source.format` and
   163	`source.version` identify the exact input grammar; `media_type` describes
   164	its bytes. The normalized formulas and assumptions MUST be retained with
   165	the original source. The import's version and source version need not be
   166	equal, but both MUST be preserved. Hash mismatch is invalid, even if a
   167	file with the same vendor name is otherwise available. No file path,
   168	network fetch, mutable URL, executable host callback, or environment
   169	variable is an implicit source or parameter binding.
   170	
   171	`value:null` denotes a free parameter, not zero or an inferred optimum.
   172	Bounds are inclusive, in the parameter's declared unit; null endpoints
   173	mean unbounded, and min MUST NOT exceed max. Parity other than `any`
   174	requires integer type. `code_distance` requires integer type,
   175	dimensionless unit with scale 1, and domain min at least 1; the domain
   176	MAY admit odd, even or all distances. `real` includes exact algebraic real
   177	values expressible by ClosedExpr. A parameter value is a dimensionless numeric ClosedExpr coefficient in
   178	its declared unit; the declared scale is applied exactly once. A bound
   179	integer value MUST be integral in its declared unit. Bound values MUST
   180	satisfy all domain constraints.
   181	No generic model can infer a distance from a risk budget.
   182	
   183	A scheduler/estimator MAY select a distance from the declared domain.
   184	Before it uses the value in an operational target binding it MUST write
   185	that choice into the model's parameter value and validate it, thereby
   186	creating the resolved target identity. The target parameter value/domain and target objective identify the
   187	chosen instance. The report records the actually searched domain, search
   188	method and objective as model-qualified verification.assumptions strings,
   189	with no machine-checked optimality claim inferred from that prose. A claim
   190	of optimality requires a separately published, independently checkable
   191	minimality/search witness format; this section introduces no such claim
   192	kind or verdict. Hardware and code-distance values cannot
   193	be overridden under an unchanged target_hash. Algorithm values used only
   194	in an estimate belong to that estimate's binding, as specified below.
   195	
   196	**Expression evaluation.** A bare Q is dimensionless; a typed literal has
   197	its declared unit. Parameter and quantity references denote base values
   198	with their declared dimensions. Addition, subtraction, min and max require
   199	identical dimensions; multiplication adds dimension exponents and division
   200	subtracts them. Binary operators have exactly two arguments, except ceil
   201	and sqrt which have one. `pow(base,exponent)` requires a dimensionless
   202	integral exponent; it scales the base's dimensions by that integer.
   203	Integer exponents may be positive, zero or negative; zero to zero and
   204	zero to a negative exponent are invalid. `sqrt` is the nonnegative
   205	principal root, halving all dimension exponents. `ceil` accepts only a
   206	dimensionless argument and returns a dimensionless integer. Division by
   207	zero and a negative root are invalid. Rational operations, integer powers,
   208	roots and their order comparisons denote exact real algebraic values;
   209	no binary64 evaluation or floating tolerance substitutes for them.
   210	
   211	A quantity's inferred dimensions MUST equal its declared unit's dimensions;
   212	its reported numeric value is its base value divided by that unit's scale.
   213	The dependency graph of quantity references MUST be acyclic; forward
   214	references are permitted. Parameters contain no quantity dependencies.
   215	Unknown references and cycles are invalid even if a particular invocation
   216	would multiply their result by zero. Duration, demand and count quantities
   217	must be nonnegative; concrete demand/count quantities must be integral in
   218	their declared unit. Risk quantities must be dimensionless, scale 1, and
   219	in [0,1]. Scalar quantities may be signed. All concrete results must be
   220	finite. Parameter-dependent validity conditions remain explicit symbolic
   221	obligations until discharged for the requested binding.
   222	
   223	These are model expressions, not extensions of every §8.1 expression
   224	context. No gate control count, iteration variable, theta, `eps`,
   225	`log2inv_eps`, `eps_decade` or table context is implicitly bound here.
   226	Their old AST spellings are invalid in a model expression. A source
   227	formula requiring further functions is represented by `external`, whose
   228	`location` identifies the exact expression in the preserved source using
   229	that source format's location convention. It has no invented numeric
   230	value. The report MUST retain that expression, identity and the unresolved
   231	interpretation/checking obligation. A pinned adapter may translate it to
   232	the exact AST above; an independent checker must verify that translation
   233	before calling the result checked. Source-model correctness and physical
   234	assumptions are distinct from verification of that translation and its
   235	arithmetic. Unsupported functions MUST NOT disappear, become zero, or
   236	invalidate program execution. Complete support for promised source models
   237	remains required; an unchecked report does not discharge that requirement.
   238	
   239	**Named outputs and conversion.** `map` has exactly one entry for every
   240	normalized quantity, using the §8.3 pair shape `quantity:[resource,factor]`.
   241	In this typed form factor is either P (a rational string) or a positive
   242	finite JSON number interpreted by exact decimal text. Rational strings
   243	allow exact conversions such as 1/3; no decimal approximation is required.
   244	The legacy untyped form retains its existing JSON-number factor. In this typed form it MUST equal source-unit scale divided by
   245	destination-resource scale, and dimensions MUST agree. Additional
   246	multiplicity is expressed in the quantity formula, not hidden in this
   247	unit conversion. Several quantities MAY map to one resource with aligned dimensions; each
   248	remains separately identified by model and quantity name, without automatic
   249	summation of their displayed values.
   250	Any quantity without an existing aligned resource MUST receive a newly
   251	declared named resource of kind `model` with the appropriate unit metadata;
   252	it MUST NOT be silently dropped or coerced to an unrelated unit. A count
   253	or risk quantity MAY instead map to a count resource, but is charged only
   254	when explicitly present in a leaf's `counts`. Duration and demand are not
   255	made additive by naming them resources. The report preserves each mapped
   256	quantity independently of whether any target field consumes it.
   257	
   258	**Operational bindings.** A binding path is a canonical RFC 6901 JSON
   259	Pointer into the target document, using `~0`/`~1` escaping and canonical
   260	nonnegative decimal array indices. It MUST name an existing field that
   261	the target schema makes numeric or a §8.1 numeric expression. Missing
   262	fields, duplicate paths, ancestor/descendant path conflicts and pointers
   263	into imports themselves are invalid. Every path identifies one field;
   264	array matching by opcode or vendor name is forbidden.
   265	
   266	The following are the allowed destinations and their units/constraints:
   267	
   268	| Destination | Unit and additional constraint |
   269	|---|---|
   270	| `gates[i].cost[r]`, each qram operation's `cost[r]` | Unit of declared resource r; r MUST NOT have kind model. Costs nonnegative. |
   271	| `classes[c].capacity` | Logical qubits of class c; integer or existing literal unbounded. Only numeric capacities may be bound. |
   272	| `classes[c].qubit_second`, `classes[c].dirty_rate` | Existing class occupancy multiplier, dimensionless and nonnegative. Physical/logical conversion instead uses the hierarchical interval report. |
   273	| `resources[r].rate[c]` | Resource r unit divided by (logical_qubit·s); nonnegative; only occupancy resources have this field. |
   274	| `capabilities.classical_step_time`, `capabilities.measurement.feedforward_latency` | Seconds, nonnegative. |
   275	| `capabilities.max_parallel`, `capabilities.max_predicate_depth` | Dimensionless integer, subject to their existing constraints. |

## 16. Canonical textual form (normative)
## 17. Decisions deferred to implementation, with defaults

Each item is one an implementer must decide to build; any decision is
conformant provided two implementations agree on the default below until
the item is revised. Items 1–5 are asserted non-semantic.

1. **Binary encoding.** Default: none; §16 text is the only interchange
   form.
2. **Gate-name registry.** Default: the names of §4.4 only; an unknown
   gate name is a load error.
3. **Further device models.** Default: the four of §7.3; an `effect` on
   any other device is `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED`.
4. **Mechanisation of §14.** Default: none; the reference interpreter is
   the oracle.
5. **Memory layout of the opaque C objects.** Default: opaque; only the
   listed functions touch them.
6. **Measurement-based cleanup** (§6.4): the normative fixup, its
   controlled variant and its certificate kind. Default: unitary cleanup
   (`reverse`); `capabilities.mbu` is `false`.
7. **Expansion library** (§11): the gate-level expansion below row
   level. Default: the library `ref` published by `bennett-isa-spec`
   (ripple-carry adders and comparators, linear `inrange` scans,
   one-hot address dispatch, clean scratch released in reverse order);
   a schedule that names no `expansion` binds `ref` at the ISA version
   in the object's header, with the full-library envelope hash published
   for that version in §11.1. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4]
   For a 0.9 object this is exactly
   `{"name":"ref","version":"0.9","hash":"5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197"}`,
   equally for omission and an explicit binding. [deps: ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4, ISA-11.1]
   §11.1 fixes its schema, ordinals, scratch identities, empty removable
   sets and deterministic decomposition algorithms. [deps: ISA-11.1]
   The bound catalog is published as `spec/ref-0.9.json`. The worked
   `ctl` action fixture (zsw.4) is in
   `conformance/fixtures/controlled-action-09/`: `controlled.bisa`,
   `target.json`, `leaves.json`, `schedule.json`, `frame.json` and
   `certificate.json`. It contains one exact controlled-X leaf, starts
   at time 0 and ends at time 1, with two live fast-class interface wires
   and no workspace. Its README records derivation and the pinned 0.8
   compatibility check separately from the 0.9 bindings.
8. **Enclosure procedure for transcendental entries** (§12.1). Default:
   Taylor series of sin and cos to the first term whose Lagrange
   remainder bound is below 2^−(`precision_bits` + 2), argument reduced
   modulo 2π with an outward-rounded enclosure of π.
9. **Default `epsilon_share`** (§8.1, §11) when a schedule assigns none.
   Default: the exact rational `bindings.epsilon / N`, where N is the
   number of gate operations in the bound-library expansion of one
   invocation of `bindings.region`, including every nested invocation,
   that have no exact native match as defined below. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3, ISA-11-P2]
   For this count only, an exact native match is a `gates` entry whose
   opcode, classes, control range and parameters cover the operation
   and whose `epsilon` expression is independent of `eps` and evaluates
   to zero; compute this test before the requested-accuracy filter,
   without selecting an achieved-error entry using the default share. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3]
   N counts the gate operations before target scheme expansion and
   includes operations with explicit shares as well as omitted shares;
   the root accuracy is `bindings.epsilon`, not a nested region's
   separately requested accuracy. [deps: ISA-11-P2, ISA-11-P3, ISA-8.2, ISA-8.3]
   Gates with an exact native match take a default share of zero; if
   N=0 all default shares are zero and no division is performed. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3]
   The resolved share is represented exactly as a reduced pair (p,q)
   of nonnegative numerator and positive denominator, with zero
   represented by (0,1); it is passed as that rational value to every
   §8.1 comparison, expression and accuracy bucket, without rounding
   to a finite decimal. [deps: ISA-8.1]
   This is a resolved value, not an inserted `epsilon_share` field;
   existing decimal schedule fields and the hashed schedule bytes are
   unchanged by default resolution. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-9.2]
   Explicit shares still supply `eps` by their exact value and the
   §8.1 requested-versus-achieved filter and achieved-error charge
   still apply to every resolved share. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3]
   *(non-normative witness)* At `bindings.epsilon: "0.1"`, three gates
   lacking exact native matches give N=3 and default (1,30), even if
   one gate has an explicit share; a fourth, exact-native gate takes
   zero and is excluded from N. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3, ISA-11-P2]
   With no such inexact gates N=0. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3, ISA-11-P2]
   Omitting expansion on a 0.9 object selects the full-library hash in
   item 7, not the single-qxor witness hash. [deps: ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4]
10. **`any`-class resolution** (§11). Default: the first class in JCS
    order with capacity.
11. **Occupancy rate of a class absent from `rate`** (§8.2). Default: 1.
12. **Classical step timing of CC/R instructions and effects** (§8.2).
    Default: `classical_step_time` each, sequential.
13. **Suite sizes** (§15): the exhaustive widths of suite C and the
    sample counts of `sampled` claims. Default: exhaustive for ≤ 16
    interface bits; 2^16 samples above.
14. **Hardware handling of `qreg.free`** (§3.3). Cleanliness of an
    arbitrary state is not testable nondestructively. Default: on
    hardware the release proceeds without a test and its frame is
    marked unverified, so a segment containing it cannot be uncalled
    (§7.4) and a run containing it is not reverse-eligible (§10);
    releasing a register that was not clean is a program error the
    hardware does not detect *(non-normative: physically its wires are
    then discarded as by an unrecorded measurement; this is a program
    error, not a trace-out operation, §10)*. The same holds for
    `bennett_qreg_prepare` and `bennett_qreg_free` on a non-verifying
    executor. A verifying executor tests and records `FAULT_UB_TRAP` on
    failure (§4.9), or returns `E_QREG_NOT_CLEAN` from the host calls.
15. **Routing and movement cost under a topology** (§8.2–§8.3, §11),
    deferred to 0.10. Default: no routing cost is charged and no routing
    is inserted; a schedule that needs a non-adjacent pair is
    `E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` naming this item. Absent topology remains
    all-to-all. A producer can supply an explicitly routed gate sequence
    using §4.4 `swap` gates; those gates execute and are charged normally.
    The optional topology schema and adjacency validation are decided in
    0.9; only automatic routing and its movement-cost model are deferred.
16. **Exhaustion of an executor's own classical resources in CC/R**
    (activation stack, history storage; §4.9). CC and R carry no budget
    (§5.4 budgets are Q-tier), so where a finite executor runs out is
    its own limit. Default: a terminal fault record `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP`
    at the instruction that cannot proceed, raised before any mutation
    by that instruction (§4.9; an executor that cannot guarantee this
    for an exhaustion inside a region call marks the run not
    reverse-eligible); the point is executor-defined and the suites do
    not test it.
17. **Identity-idle step** (§3.6). A variant of the step in which an
    idle step leaves its frame at zero: forward, a = (pc ≠ HALT) into
# Bennett ISA reference interpreter

Rust 2021, standard library only. ISA 0.8 remains supported; ISA 0.9 migration
is in progress. Complete ISA 0.9 execution is not yet claimed. [PROGRESS.md](PROGRESS.md) records
what is verified, what is staged and in what order; [SPEC-QUESTIONS.md](SPEC-QUESTIONS.md)
records every reading the specification leaves open;
[council/response-round-1.md](council/response-round-1.md) answers the council's
round-1 review blocker by blocker. The crate is not yet a complete conformance
reference: the launch ABI (§10), target descriptions (§8), the schedule
consumer (§11) and the rigorous interval certificate checker (§12.1) are staged
as deliverables 8–11, and claims that need them are reported as unverified,
never as verified.

Build and test offline from the repository root (headless: `DISPLAY`,
`WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS`, `BROWSER` unset;
`TMPDIR=$PWD/reference-interpreter/.tmp`, created first):

```sh
cargo build --manifest-path reference-interpreter/Cargo.toml --offline
cargo test --manifest-path reference-interpreter/Cargo.toml --offline
cargo test --manifest-path reference-interpreter/Cargo.toml --offline -- --ignored
```

The executable is `reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref`. Data
commands write one JSON object to stdout and exit 0; `help`/`--help` prints
plain usage. Diagnostics go to stderr with exit status 1, and ISA diagnostics
cite their rule. Integers above 2^53 − 1 are decimal strings (§9.1).

> **Council status (2026-10-31):** accepted by the sol and codex seats in round 24 of run `isa-ref-2026-10-29` on snapshot ad84e75 (461 tests, five exhaustive sweeps; rounds 1–24 under `bennett/docs/council/isa-ref-2026-10-29/`). Staged deliverables 8–11 remain disclosed as staged. Muse seat held.

## Typed R faults and contract migration (ISA-7)

ISA 0.9 already resolves b-isa-spec-221: §0.2 K5, §4.1 `fault`,
§4.9 (implicit cause table, exact 64-bit terminal code, no successor or
later source effect, intact prefix history), §7.4, §10 (FAILED → REVERSED,
no result values, one fault record) and §14.3 T4. Zero maps to code 4;
nonzero codes, including program-defined codes, are preserved exactly.
This is a published 0.9 contract, not an amendment to accepted ISA 0.8.

The dialect 0.2.10 at `023f5a7` §11.5/§17.6 correctly reports
`blocked:BD-B-ISA7` for every R entry targeting ISA 0.8: the mandatory
pre-instruction source step check needs `fault 1` before the instruction,
with the same effect-tape prefix and reversible history. `--budget` here
is host execution fuel, not that source counter; generic `trap` cannot
implement arbitrary typed R faults. Closing the ISA wording issue does
not clear that consumer blocker or certify rung 3. Open follow-up
`b-isa-spec-fuk` retains that producer/executor migration prerequisite.

The reference migration has landed explicit 0.8/0.9 envelope, ABI, identity,
linking and device-version dispatch, plus K5 typed terminal faults and eligible
FAILED-run reversal on 0.9. `tests/typed_faults.rs` checks source-instrumented
pre-instruction boundaries (zero and returns included), exact uint64 codes,
implicit causes, intact prefixes and entry restoration. The source counter
remains producer instrumentation; host fuel is a separate diagnostic.

Bound `ref` expansion, end-to-end K79–K81 execution, the 0.9 saved-tape matrix
and the remaining authoritative delta (including dynamic qregs, minted tokens,
parametrised qinplace and nonempty lproc forwarding) remain open under
`b-isa-spec-fuk.3`–`.7`. The producer and sibling contract pins have not been
migrated here. The full musl hello-world, native equality, R console, reset
and saved-tape replay acceptance remains owed (North Star §8 rung 3;
dialect §19.2). The council status above applies only to its pinned snapshot;
a new milestone acceptance round remains the orchestrator's responsibility.

    95	exponents are admitted. Dimensionless units have `{}`. Unit metadata is

    97

    99	`dimension:{S:Q}` and `scale:P` in addition to its §8.2 `unit:string`.
   100	Its `unit` remains the display label. These two fields are optional and
   101	have no effect for resources not participating in this section. The

   117

   128	weighted/lexicographic rules then apply to that scalar. An unbound
   129	objective is retained symbolically and has no silently assigned scalar
   130	value or completed concrete optimization verdict.
   131	
   132	**Normalized model schema.** The import key supplies the model name.
   133	Names of units, parameters, quantities and nodes are local to that model.
   134	`model_hash` is SHA-256 of UTF-8 JCS bytes of the complete `model` object,
   135	without a final LF; `source.sha256` below hashes the decoded raw bytes.
   136	
   137	```text
   138	model = {
   139	  schema: "bennett.import-model/1",
   140	  source: {format:S,version:S,media_type:S,bytes:Hex,sha256:H},
   141	  units: {S:Unit},
   142	  parameters: {S:Parameter},
   143	  quantities: {S:Quantity},
   144	  bindings: [Binding,...],
   145	  nodes: {S:Node},
   146	  assumptions: [S,...]
   147	}
   148	Parameter = {
   149	  role: "hardware" | "code_distance" | "algorithm" | "other",
   150	  type: "integer" | "real", unit:S, value: ClosedExpr | null,
   151	  domain: {min:Q|null,max:Q|null,parity:"any"|"odd"|"even"}
   152	}
   153	Quantity = {unit:S,role:"duration"|"demand"|"risk"|"count"|"scalar",expr:Expr}
   154	Binding = {path:S,quantity:S,factor:P,rounding:"exact"|"upper"|"lower",places:N,value:Q|null}
   155	Expr = Q | {lit:Q,unit:S} | {param:S} | {quantity:S}
   156	     | {op:Op,args:[Expr,...]}
   157	     | {external:{location:S,unit:S}}
   158	Op = "+" | "-" | "*" | "/" | "min" | "max" | "ceil" | "pow" | "sqrt"
   159	ClosedExpr = Expr with no param, quantity or external reference
   160	```
   161	
   162	Every unit reference MUST resolve in `units`. `source.format` and
   163	`source.version` identify the exact input grammar; `media_type` describes
   164	its bytes. The normalized formulas and assumptions MUST be retained with
   165	the original source. The import's version and source version need not be
   166	equal, but both MUST be preserved. Hash mismatch is invalid, even if a
   167	file with the same vendor name is otherwise available. No file path,
   168	network fetch, mutable URL, executable host callback, or environment
   169	variable is an implicit source or parameter binding.
   170	
   171	`value:null` denotes a free parameter, not zero or an inferred optimum.
   172	Bounds are inclusive, in the parameter's declared unit; null endpoints
   173	mean unbounded, and min MUST NOT exceed max. Parity other than `any`
   174	requires integer type. `code_distance` requires integer type,
   175	dimensionless unit with scale 1, and domain min at least 1; the domain
   176	MAY admit odd, even or all distances. `real` includes exact algebraic real
   177	values expressible by ClosedExpr. A parameter value is a dimensionless numeric ClosedExpr coefficient in
   178	its declared unit; the declared scale is applied exactly once. A bound
   179	integer value MUST be integral in its declared unit. Bound values MUST
   180	satisfy all domain constraints.
   181	No generic model can infer a distance from a risk budget.
   182	
   183	A scheduler/estimator MAY select a distance from the declared domain.
   184	Before it uses the value in an operational target binding it MUST write
   185	that choice into the model's parameter value and validate it, thereby
   186	creating the resolved target identity. The target parameter value/domain and target objective identify the
   187	chosen instance. The report records the actually searched domain, search
   188	method and objective as model-qualified verification.assumptions strings,
   189	with no machine-checked optimality claim inferred from that prose. A claim
   190	of optimality requires a separately published, independently checkable
   191	minimality/search witness format; this section introduces no such claim
   192	kind or verdict. Hardware and code-distance values cannot
   193	be overridden under an unchanged target_hash. Algorithm values used only
   194	in an estimate belong to that estimate's binding, as specified below.
   195	
   196	**Expression evaluation.** A bare Q is dimensionless; a typed literal has
   197	its declared unit. Parameter and quantity references denote base values
   198	with their declared dimensions. Addition, subtraction, min and max require
   199	identical dimensions; multiplication adds dimension exponents and division
   200	subtracts them. Binary operators have exactly two arguments, except ceil
   201	and sqrt which have one. `pow(base,exponent)` requires a dimensionless
   202	integral exponent; it scales the base's dimensions by that integer.
   203	Integer exponents may be positive, zero or negative; zero to zero and
   204	zero to a negative exponent are invalid. `sqrt` is the nonnegative
   205	principal root, halving all dimension exponents. `ceil` accepts only a
   206	dimensionless argument and returns a dimensionless integer. Division by
   207	zero and a negative root are invalid. Rational operations, integer powers,
   208	roots and their order comparisons denote exact real algebraic values;
   209	no binary64 evaluation or floating tolerance substitutes for them.
   210	
   211	A quantity's inferred dimensions MUST equal its declared unit's dimensions;
   212	its reported numeric value is its base value divided by that unit's scale.
   213	The dependency graph of quantity references MUST be acyclic; forward
   214	references are permitted. Parameters contain no quantity dependencies.
   215	Unknown references and cycles are invalid even if a particular invocation
   216	would multiply their result by zero. Duration, demand and count quantities
   217	must be nonnegative; concrete demand/count quantities must be integral in
   218	their declared unit. Risk quantities must be dimensionless, scale 1, and
   219	in [0,1]. Scalar quantities may be signed. All concrete results must be
   220	finite. Parameter-dependent validity conditions remain explicit symbolic
   221	obligations until discharged for the requested binding.
   222	
   223	These are model expressions, not extensions of every §8.1 expression
   224	context. No gate control count, iteration variable, theta, `eps`,
   225	`log2inv_eps`, `eps_decade` or table context is implicitly bound here.
   226	Their old AST spellings are invalid in a model expression. A source
   227	formula requiring further functions is represented by `external`, whose
   228	`location` identifies the exact expression in the preserved source using
   229	that source format's location convention. It has no invented numeric
   230	value. The report MUST retain that expression, identity and the unresolved
   231	interpretation/checking obligation. A pinned adapter may translate it to
   232	the exact AST above; an independent checker must verify that translation
   233	before calling the result checked. Source-model correctness and physical
   234	assumptions are distinct from verification of that translation and its
   235	arithmetic. Unsupported functions MUST NOT disappear, become zero, or
   236	invalidate program execution. Complete support for promised source models
   237	remains required; an unchecked report does not discharge that requirement.
   238	
   239	**Named outputs and conversion.** `map` has exactly one entry for every
   240	normalized quantity, using the §8.3 pair shape `quantity:[resource,factor]`.
   241	In this typed form factor is either P (a rational string) or a positive
   242	finite JSON number interpreted by exact decimal text. Rational strings
   243	allow exact conversions such as 1/3; no decimal approximation is required.
   244	The legacy untyped form retains its existing JSON-number factor. In this typed form it MUST equal source-unit scale divided by
   245	destination-resource scale, and dimensions MUST agree. Additional
   246	multiplicity is expressed in the quantity formula, not hidden in this
   247	unit conversion. Several quantities MAY map to one resource with aligned dimensions; each
   248	remains separately identified by model and quantity name, without automatic
   249	summation of their displayed values.
   250	Any quantity without an existing aligned resource MUST receive a newly
   251	declared named resource of kind `model` with the appropriate unit metadata;
   252	it MUST NOT be silently dropped or coerced to an unrelated unit. A count
   253	or risk quantity MAY instead map to a count resource, but is charged only
   254	when explicitly present in a leaf's `counts`. Duration and demand are not
   255	made additive by naming them resources. The report preserves each mapped
   256	quantity independently of whether any target field consumes it.
   257	
   258	**Operational bindings.** A binding path is a canonical RFC 6901 JSON
   259	Pointer into the target document, using `~0`/`~1` escaping and canonical
   260	nonnegative decimal array indices. It MUST name an existing field that
   261	the target schema makes numeric or a §8.1 numeric expression. Missing
   262	fields, duplicate paths, ancestor/descendant path conflicts and pointers
   263	into imports themselves are invalid. Every path identifies one field;
   264	array matching by opcode or vendor name is forbidden.
   265	
   266	The following are the allowed destinations and their units/constraints:
   267	
   268	| Destination | Unit and additional constraint |
   269	|---|---|
   270	| `gates[i].cost[r]`, each qram operation's `cost[r]` | Unit of declared resource r; r MUST NOT have kind model. Costs nonnegative. |
   271	| `classes[c].capacity` | Logical qubits of class c; integer or existing literal unbounded. Only numeric capacities may be bound. |
   272	| `classes[c].qubit_second`, `classes[c].dirty_rate` | Existing class occupancy multiplier, dimensionless and nonnegative. Physical/logical conversion instead uses the hierarchical interval report. |
   273	| `resources[r].rate[c]` | Resource r unit divided by (logical_qubit·s); nonnegative; only occupancy resources have this field. |
   274	| `capabilities.classical_step_time`, `capabilities.measurement.feedforward_latency` | Seconds, nonnegative. |
   275	| `capabilities.max_parallel`, `capabilities.max_predicate_depth` | Dimensionless integer, subject to their existing constraints. |
   276	| `gates[i].controls.min/max`, `schemes[i].realises.controls.min/max` | Dimensionless integer, existing inclusive-range constraints. |
   277	| `schemes[i].ancillae.count` | Logical qubits, nonnegative integer. |
   278	
   279	For all other quantities the named-model-output path remains available;
   280	new numeric destination families require their own published schema and
   281	unit rule, never an implicit substitution. This table does not authorize
   282	binding an epsilon, boolean capability, topology, quantum operation,
   283	interface width, source budget or certificate proof field. Imported
   284	physical risk MUST NOT be substituted for gate epsilon, diamond norm,
   285	operator norm, oracle fault rate, or any correctness assertion.
   286	
   287	The binding's local `quantity` MUST resolve. Its dimensions MUST equal
   288	the destination's; `factor` MUST equal source-unit scale divided by the
   289	destination unit's scale. Let x be the exact quantity value times factor.
   290	For `exact`, x MUST be rational and `value` MUST equal x. For `upper`,
   291	`value=ceil(x*10^places)/10^places`; for `lower`,
   292	`value=floor(x*10^places)/10^places`. These bounds use exact comparisons,
   293	including at decimal boundaries. Nonintegral results at integral
   294	destinations are invalid. `places` MUST be zero at such destinations.
   295	Only exact binding is allowed for capability limits and control ranges;
   296	capacity may use exact or lower, and cost/duration/rate fields exact or
   297	upper. Thus rounding cannot overstate a capacity or understate a cost.
   298	
   299	A concrete binding overrides the field at its explicit path; the original
   300	field remains as serialized provenance, not a fallback if resolution fails.
   301	Every nonnull `value` MUST be independently recomputed and checked against
   302	the expression and rounding rule. Symbolic templates have `value:null`;
   303	all operational bindings must have valid nonnull values and bound
   304	parameters before concrete scheduling. Changing any operational parameter,
   305	resolved value, formula, mapping, source identity or binding changes the
   306	JCS target bytes and hence target_hash. Scheduling and launch reports bind
   307	that resolved hash; consumers MUST NOT cache under the unresolved target's
   308	hash or reuse a distance selected for another target instance.
   309	
   310	**Hierarchy node grammar.** `nodes` is a finite, acyclic map of reusable
   311	node templates. Child names refer to the same map; invoking another model
   312	is explicit. All node and reference validation precedes evaluation,
   313	including children of a zero-count repeat.
   314	
   315	```text
   316	Node = {kind:"leaf",duration:Expr,counts:{S:Expr},
   317	        intervals:[Interval,...],shared_uses:[{id:S,slots:Expr},...]}
   318	     | {kind:"sequence"|"parallel",children:[S,...]}
   319	     | {kind:"repeat",child:S,count:Expr}
   320	     | {kind:"invoke",model:S,node:S,arguments:{S:Expr}}
   321	Interval = {pool:S,unit:S,start:Expr,end:Expr,amount:Expr}
   322	```
   323	
   324	Leaf duration and interval endpoints have time dimensions and are
   325	nonnegative. Each interval is half-open `[start,end)`, relative to leaf
   326	start, with `0<=start<=end<=duration`. Amount has the interval unit's
   327	dimensions, is nonnegative, and is integral when it measures physical or
   328	logical qubits. Pool identifies a local accounting pool. Report pool keys are the JCS
   329	encoding of the two-string array [model,pool], used as a JSON object key;
   330	selector pool fields use that same qualified encoding. This avoids aliases
   331	when either name contains punctuation or Unicode. Units
   332	within a pool MUST have identical dimensions and convert to a common
   333	base. Counts name declared count resources and have their dimensions;
   334	values are converted from base units to the resource unit before summing.
   335	Negative charges or any automatic probability composition are forbidden.
   336	Physical risk summed as a charge is an additive model number, possibly
   337	above one, not a declared failure probability or error guarantee.
   338	
   339	Sequence places its children consecutively, starting at zero; parallel
   340	places every child at zero; children may repeat and each occurrence is a
   341	separate invocation, not memoized-away work. Empty compositions have zero
   342	duration, counts, occupancy and peak. Repeat concatenates count copies;
   343	count is a dimensionless nonnegative integer, with zero giving the empty
   344	composition. Invoke instantiates the referenced model's node, retaining
   345	its source/units/assumptions. Its arguments are evaluated in the calling model's environment and bind
   346	only the referenced model's algorithm parameters, with matching dimensions
   347	and conversion to the callee's declared units. They must satisfy the
   348	callee's types/domains; free caller parameters remain symbolic. Other
   349	parameters retain resolved target values. Unspecified algorithm arguments
   350	inherit the report's binding for that model. Dependency cycles, including
   351	cross-model invokes, are invalid; arbitrarily large finite repeats MAY
   352	be evaluated symbolically without materializing their leaves.
   353	
   354	Every private interval is instantiated separately at its occurrence's
   355	time origin. Count resources sum once per leaf occurrence. Duration is

   350	inherit the report's binding for that model. Dependency cycles, including
   351	cross-model invokes, are invalid; arbitrarily large finite repeats MAY
   352	be evaluated symbolically without materializing their leaves.
   353	
   354	Every private interval is instantiated separately at its occurrence's
   355	time origin. Count resources sum once per leaf occurrence. Duration is
   356	the largest leaf end after composition. Pool occupancy is the integral of
   357	simultaneous demand over time; pool peak is its maximum. Concurrent demands
   358	sum, and touching half-open intervals do not overlap. The result MUST be
   359	identical to explicit expansion at any bound finite input, but expansion
   360	is not required. No root total is obtained by summing child peaks or by
   361	adding parent resource totals to their child totals.
   362	
   363	Shared resources are declared once by the report's `shared` array, in
   364	root time coordinates. A declaration has exactly
   365	`{id:S,model:S,pool:S,unit:S,start:Expr,end:Expr,amount:Expr,slots:P}`;
   366	model selects the expression/unit namespace, slots is a positive integer,
   367	and other dimensional rules match Interval. Its interval must lie within
   368	the root duration. A leaf shared-use id names that declaration and uses
   369	a dimensionless integral nonnegative number of slots for its full leaf duration. The
   370	reservation must cover that use, and the simultaneous slot demand must
   371	never exceed its slots. Its physical amount contributes **once** to
   372	pool occupancy and peak, independent of the number of references. A
   373	private interval and a shared declaration cannot claim the same physical
   374	allocation; the producer MUST choose one ownership representation and
   375	provide that ownership in its model assumptions. An idle reserved factory
   376	may still consume physical space. Exclusive factories use slots=1;
   377	parallel uses needing two slots are infeasible until separately reserved
   378	capacity or serialization is supplied. No duplicate shared id is allowed.
   379	
   380	**Reports, identity and checking.** Add optional `model_estimates:[Report,...]`
   381	to a §12.3 region record. Existing region resources remain the §8.2
   382	scheduled-leaf report. The additional report has this exact schema:
   383	
   384	```text
   385	Report = {
   386	  schema:"bennett.model-estimate/1", target_hash:H,
   387	  subject:{closure_hash:H,invocation:Invocation,context?:ContextKey},
   388	  models:{S:{model_hash:H,parameters:{S:ClosedExpr|null}}},
   389	  root:{model:S,node:S}, shared:[Shared,...],
   390	  outputs:{duration:Result,counts:{S:Result},
   391	           occupancy:{S:{unit:Unit,value:Result}},
   392	           peak:{S:{unit:Unit,value:Result}},
   393	           quantities:{S:{S:{resource:S,value:Result}}}},
   394	  verification:{status:"checked"|"symbolic"|"unchecked",
   395	                obligations:[{model:S,location:S,reason:S},...],
   396	                assumptions:[{model:S,text:S},...]}
   397	}
   398	Result = {exact:ClosedExpr}
   399	       | {symbolic:{model:S,node:S,metric:S,pool?:S,resource?:S,quantity?:S}}
   400	       | {interval:{lo:Q,hi:Q,places:N}}
   401	```
   402	
   403	Invocation and ContextKey are the existing exact encodings of §12.3 and
   404	§12.1; absence of context means a standalone invocation, not the first
   405	reached context. A scheduled report MUST carry the actual context key and
   406	root schedule binding through its containing certificate. Repeated
   407	contexts cannot share one result unless independently shown equal.
   408	The models map contains exactly the transitively referenced models and
   409	their canonical model_hash values. Its parameters map lists every parameter
   410	exactly once in its declared unit. Hardware/code-distance/other parameters
   411	MUST equal target values; only algorithm parameters may replace null or
   412	algorithm defaults in this report. A parameter's replacement also applies
   413	to all direct uses of that model unless an invoke explicitly binds another
   414	algorithm value. Such per-invoke arguments are part of the checked DAG,
   415	not one misleading global multiplicity. No coherent state or qubit
   416	measurement may supply a parameter during a coherent region.
   417	
   418	Duration is reported in seconds; counts in their target resource units;
   419	peak in the displayed Unit of the pool, occupancy in that Unit times
   420	seconds. The quantity outputs contain, for every referenced model and
   421	every mapped quantity keyed by its local name, its destination resource
   422	name and converted value, in the report-level parameter environment. Locally rebound invocations are
   423	additionally identified by their DAG occurrence when requested individually;
   424	their aggregate contribution follows hierarchy composition, not that
   425	report-level quantity display. Missing metric keys are zero only when
   426	recomputation establishes there is no contribution. Foreign or additional
   427	asserted outputs must be checked, never silently ignored.
   428	
   429	An exact result uses a dimensionless ClosedExpr for the numeric value in
   430	the stated output unit. Interval endpoints are rational in that same
   431	unit, lo<=hi; at `places` the checker requires the tight outward decimal
   432	floor and ceiling of the exact result. An interval is a bound, not an
   433	exact point. A symbolic result is a thunk for the specified root/node
   434	metric under the report bindings; metric is exactly `duration`, `counts`,
   435	`occupancy`, `peak` or `quantity`. Counts requires resource, quantity
   436	requires quantity, occupancy/peak require pool, and all other optional
   437	selector fields are forbidden. Model and node must resolve. It retains the entire dependent expression/hierarchy and
   438	all domain obligations, including min/max and interval overlaps; no
   439	unsupported scalar expression is fabricated. Free parameters may stay
   440	null in a symbolic report, including an unresolved hardware design study.
   441	Such a report cannot discharge a concrete operational binding.
   442	
   443	The checker verifies all hashes, typed bindings, graph references, domain
   444	conditions, conversions, rounding, multiplicities, reservations, outputs
   445	and context identity independently. `checked` means these obligations
   446	succeeded for concrete parameters; it does not establish that an external
   447	physical QEC model is true. `symbolic` means structural/unit checks
   448	succeeded and unresolved items are exactly the free-parameter obligations.
   449	`unchecked` is required if any translation, external-function or requested
   450	verification obligation remains unsupported; every such obligation MUST
   451	appear with its model, source/AST location and reason. All imported
   452	assumptions appear in the report's assumptions, qualified by model; they
   453	are not proof labels. A claimed checked status with a missing obligation
   454	is invalid. Results do not become permutation proofs, channel bounds,
   455	operator-norm bounds or oracle fault guarantees by appearing in a checked
   456	model report.
   457	
   458	Malformed typed input, conflicting binding or a mathematically invalid
   459	concrete expression produces an explicit model/report validation error
   460	with the failed location. An unresolved operational binding prevents a
   461	concrete schedule, with the missing parameter/expression reported. A
   462	capacity/slot-constrained candidate schedule is infeasible with
   463	`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` under the existing schedule interface, which permits another schedule. None of these creates
   464	a new ISA fault code, changes F_B, rejects the program as outside the
   465	language, erases an earlier valid loaded state, or licenses a fallback to
   466	zero or a vendor default. An unused or unresolved analytical model does
   467	not prevent execution under an independently valid concrete target.
   468	Unsupported source-format interpretation yields a preserved unchecked
   469	model plus its explicit implementation obligation; it is not a successful
   470	estimate or permission to abandon the promised adapter capability.
   471	
   472	End normative insertion.
   473	
   474	### Corresponding §12.3 insertion (proposed normative text)
   475	
   476	A region MAY carry `model_estimates` as specified in §8.5. Those reports
   477	are separate, explicitly identified accounting views. They MUST NOT be
   478	added to the region's required §8.2 scheduled-leaf resources, charged again
   479	as parent-plus-child costs, or substituted for the region's logical error
   480	or fault-rate obligations. An invocation's selected QEC distance and
   481	hardware binding affect target_hash; algorithm inputs and hierarchy
   482	reports affect certificate bytes. Physical failure charges and all
   483	physical-model assumptions remain separate from epsilon and correctness
   484	claims. No model output changes the object's instruction semantics.

   486	## Worked serialized import
   487	
   488	This is the **complete** `imports.surface` entry, not a complete target
   489	or an accepted vendor fixture. It is pretty-printed for reading; the complete target is canonicalized
   490	with RFC 8785 before identity calculation. Its preserved source is a small illustrative
   491	formula JSON, with explicit normalized AST. It is not an Azure document
   492	and no vendor adapter was run. The earlier fd0 example's formulas are
   493	retained: d=5, tau=2 ns, p=1/1000, pth=1/10. A code with even distances
   494	would use another declared parity/domain, not change generic validation.
   495	
   496	Required target context: a native gate at `gates[0]` has `cost.time:0`
   497	(the explicit binding overrides this provenance value) and `epsilon:0`.
   498	Declare `time` with dimension `{s:"1"}`, scale `"1"`;
   499	`model_cycle` (kind model, seconds); `model_physical_ratio` (kind model,
   500	physical_qubit/logical_qubit); and `risk_charge` (kind count, dimensionless),
   501	all with the metadata above. Retain normal mandatory logical occupancy
   502	and peak declarations. Model hierarchy pool `["surface","data"]` is physical;
   503	it does not replace the logical register ledger or class capacity.

**Schedule document** (JCS) has the following schema, including reserved
support points and optional instance/call-occurrence selectors. [deps: ISA-11-P2, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-11.1-P22, ISA-11.1-P29]

```
  { "bindings": { "closure_hash", "target_hash", "region", "variant", "inherited_controls", "budget": [B,heap,stack,depth],
                  "params_hash", "epsilon", "expansion": { "name", "version", "hash" }, "scheduler": { "name", "version" } },
    "interface_vertices"?: [ [v, …], … ],
    "entries": [ { "path": [ { "symbol": hex, "point": "<block>:<ordinal>" | "$construct" | "$step" | "$commit" | "$release", "iteration": [n, …] | "*",
                              "instance"?: [n, …] | "*", "call_index"?: n | "*" }, … ],
                   "step": n | "*",  "phase": "fwd" | "rev" | "*",  "generated": n | "*",
                   "kind": "cleanup" | "gate" | "alloc" | "start",
                   "cleanup": { "realisation": "reverse" | "recompute" },
                   "gate":    { "realisation": "native" | "<scheme name>", "epsilon_share": decimal },
                   "alloc":   { "class": "<cls>", "vertices"?: [v, …] },           /* a concrete class; "any" is not allowed in a schedule */
                   "start":   { "time": decimal } } ] }
```

**Topology placement.** With a §8.3 topology, `interface_vertices`
lists inherited control wires first as individual width-one arrays, then
one vertex array per source interface slot in §9.4 slot order; array
elements follow bit order (and region cell address then bit order). A callee inherits its actual
operand placements. `alloc.vertices`, when present, assigns every bit
of that allocation in bit order. These arrays MUST have the declared
width, resolve to the correct classes and have disjoint live uses;
without a topology they MUST be absent. Omitted placements use the
lowest-index free vertex of the resolved class, assigning bits in order;
interface slots are assigned first in slot order, then allocations in
generated order. Vertices are retained for a wire's lifetime and freed
on release; borrowed wires retain their owner's vertices. Every native
leaf, including scheme leaves, MUST satisfy §8.3 adjacency, else
`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` naming §17 item 15. No routing is inserted.

**Matching.** Every generated operation has an **invocation chain**:
the program points of the region calls and `lift.oracle_call`s that led
to it, outermost first, ending in its own point. [deps: ISA-4.7, ISA-4.2, ISA-3.6]
`lift.call` adds no element: an lproc row in an oracle's closure generates
its expansion once per fixed row instance per step under its dispatch
predicate, independently of which `lift.call` reached that instance or
the recursion depth. [deps: ISA-3.6, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6]
`path` matches
an operation iff it is a **prefix** of that chain, element by element:
`symbol` and `point` equal, and `iteration` either `*` or equal to the
vector of 0-based dynamic repetition counts of the `qloop`s enclosing
that point (outermost first; its length is the point's loop nesting
depth, `[]` outside every loop); `instance` and `call_index` match as
specified below. [deps: ISA-4.6, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11.1-P9]
An oracle invocation's forward step coordinate is t ∈ 1..B and its
inverse step t has `phase: rev`; the root B is `bindings.budget[0]`
and a nested oracle's B is the steps component of its own call token,
not the parent's B. [deps: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-5.4, ISA-10]
An entry's `step` and `phase` read the oracle invocation entered by the
last oracle-call element of its `path`, or the root invocation if there
is no such element. [deps: ISA-4.2, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.2-P3]
A call element enters that invocation only when it is followed along
