"""Generate the complete draft catalog or historical family checkpoints. Python -I, stdlib only.
No interpreter imports. Does not publish or update the normative hash marker.
"""
import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import runpy
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
def jcs(v):
    # This catalog uses integer numbers below 2**53; no floats. UTF-16 key order.
    if type(v) is bool: return "true" if v else "false"
    if isinstance(v, dict):
        return '{' + ','.join(jcs(k)+':'+jcs(v[k]) for k in sorted(v, key=lambda k:k.encode('utf-16be'))) + '}'
    if isinstance(v, list):
        return '[' + ','.join(map(jcs,v)) + ']'
    if isinstance(v, str):
        return json.dumps(v, ensure_ascii=False, separators=(',', ':'))
    if type(v) is int and abs(v) < 2**53:
        return str(v)
    raise ValueError(f'outside catalog JCS subset: {v!r}')
def plus(a,b): return ['+',a,b]
def minus(a,b): return ['-',a,b]
def times(a,b): return ['*',a,b]
def wire(name, bit=0): return name+'['+jcs(bit)+']'
def gate(g,*w): return {'gate':g,'wires':list(w)}
def alloc(n,w): return {'alloc':n,'width':w,'class':'any','state':'clean'}
def release(n): return {'release':n}
def loop(i,n,b): return {'for':i,'from':0,'to':n,'body':b}
def inv(b): return {'inverse':True,'body':b}
class Circuit:
    def __init__(self): self.body=[]; self.scratch=[]; self.serial=0
    def temp(self,width=1):
        n=f'z{self.serial}'; self.serial+=1
        self.scratch.append(n); self.body.append(alloc(n,width)); return n
    def bit(self): return wire(self.temp())
    def boolean(self,e,out):
        """Postorder tree, fresh scratch per node, no CSE; root XOR out."""
        computes=[]; names=[]
        def visit(e):
            if isinstance(e,str): return e
            op,*args=e
            if op=='or': return visit(('xor',('xor',*args),('and',*args)))
            ws=[visit(a) for a in args]
            n=self.temp(); names.append(n); d=wire(n)
            if op=='not': ops=[gate('x',d),gate('cx',ws[0],d)]
            elif op=='xor': ops=[gate('cx',s,d) for s in ws]
            elif op=='and': ops=[gate('cx',ws[0],d)] if ws[0]==ws[1] else [gate('ccx',*ws,d)]
            else: raise ValueError(op)
            self.body.extend(ops); computes.extend(ops); return d
        root=visit(e); self.body.append(gate('cx',root,out)); self.body.append(inv(computes))
        self.body.extend(release(n) for n in reversed(names))
    def ripple(self,a,b,n,sub=False):
        p=self.temp(n); s=self.temp(n); c=self.temp(plus(n,1))
# The council

A loop in which three models grade a document against
`docs/council/rubric.md`, the maintainer's editor
revises the document on their blocking issues, and the loop repeats until
every member accepts, or three rounds have run. The loop shape is borrowed
from the `council` skill in t3dotgg/tstack ("gets review from three models
from different providers, and loops until they agree") and from the design
council in `../sublimite/docs/design/council.md`. The rubric and the
resumable runner are ours.

Scores are advisory. The maintainer decides.

## Members

| Member | Call | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Sol (`sol`) `gpt-6.1-sol`, reasoning `xhigh` | `codex exec -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort=xhigh -s read-only --ephemeral --output-schema … -o …` | Substitutes for the Opus/Fable seat in the current council. Same CLI construction as `codex`; the prompt goes on stdin. |
| Codex (`codex`) `gpt-6-astra`, reasoning `xhigh` | `codex exec -m gpt-6-astra -c model_reasoning_effort=xhigh -s read-only --ephemeral --output-schema … -o …` | The runner sends the prompt on stdin and closes it. Run from the repo so the read-only sandbox can read it. |
| Muse `muse-wonder-full-1.0`, reasoning `high` | `muse exec --model muse-wonder-full-1.0 --reasoning-effort high --output-schema … --prompt-file …` | Prints status lines before the JSON; the runner takes the last JSON object. |
| Claude Opus 5.5 (`opus`, legacy) | `claude -p --model opus --output-format json --json-schema … --allowedTools Read Grep Glob` with the prompt on stdin | Nested inside a Claude Code session, so the runner unsets `CLAUDECODE*` in the child's environment. The prompt goes on stdin because `--allowedTools` is variadic and swallows a trailing prompt argument. |
| Claude Fable (`fable`, legacy) | `claude -p --model fable --output-format json --json-schema … --allowedTools …` with the prompt on stdin | Also permits the existing implementation-review test commands. |

Each member may read the repository (read-only) to check the document's
claims; the document itself is embedded verbatim in the prompt so a member
that reads nothing still grades the right text.

### Members run as T3 subagents

The current council is `sol,codex,muse`. An orchestrator launches one T3 Code
subagent per member using the model and reasoning effort above. `council.py`
prepares and collects their work; the orchestrator does not invoke `run`.
The CLI calls in the table remain available for legacy subprocess rounds;
the default member list is unchanged, so pass `--members sol,codex,muse`.

Use this sequence (set `repo` to the absolute checkout path):

```sh
repo=/absolute/path/to/bennett
mkdir -p "$repo/.tmp"
unset DISPLAY WAYLAND_DISPLAY DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS BROWSER
export TMPDIR="$repo/.tmp"
python3 "$repo/tools/council.py" prepare --run external-review --round 1 \
        --doc "$repo/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md" --members sol,codex,muse --cwd "$repo"
python3 "$repo/tools/council.py" prompt-path --run external-review --round 1
# Line 1 is the absolute prompt.md path; line 2 is the subagents' cwd.
# Orchestrator: launch one T3 Code subagent each for sol, codex and muse.
# Give each prompt.md and the adjacent schema.json, using the printed cwd.
# Tell each to review read-only and write only its JSON result to its own file:
# sol -> $TMPDIR/sol.json; codex -> $TMPDIR/codex.json; muse -> $TMPDIR/muse.json.
# Wait for all three files, then collect sequentially:
python3 "$repo/tools/council.py" collect --run external-review --round 1 --member sol --file "$TMPDIR/sol.json"
python3 "$repo/tools/council.py" collect --run external-review --round 1 --member codex --file "$TMPDIR/codex.json"
python3 "$repo/tools/council.py" collect --run external-review --round 1 --member muse --file "$TMPDIR/muse.json"
python3 "$repo/tools/council.py" aggregate --run external-review --round 1
```

`collect` uses the same output parser and validation as `run`, including the
round's saved `schema.json`. Each collection records `raw-<member>-<attempt>.txt`
with an external-source note, atomically publishes valid `out-<member>.json`,
and updates `state.json` (start time from the source file's mtime, elapsed
seconds to collection, update time now). Invalid input fails loudly and is
recorded as a failed attempt; any previously valid output remains intact.

## Resumability

Everything lives under `docs/council/<run>/round-<N>/` inside the
repository, never in a session-scoped temp directory, so a crashed Claude
session, a dropped network, or a provider capacity failure loses nothing:

- `prepare` snapshots the document (`doc.md` plus its SHA-256 in
  `state.json`), writes `prompt.md` and `schema.json`. Re-running `prepare`
  on a round that already has member output refuses unless `--force`.
- `run` launches every member that has no valid `out-<member>.json`, in
  parallel, each with a wall-clock timeout, retrying on failure with
  backoff (60 s, 180 s, 420 s) up to four attempts. Every attempt's stdout
  and stderr are kept (`raw-<member>-<attempt>.txt`). Output is validated
  against the schema before it counts; an invalid answer is a failed
  attempt. `state.json` is rewritten atomically after every state change,
  so `status` always tells the truth and `run` is safe to invoke again at
  any time.
- `aggregate` validates all outputs, writes `report.md` and
  `consensus.json` (`{"agree": bool, "verdicts": …, "blocking": …}`).
- `status` prints the state of every round of a run.

## Run

```sh
python3 tools/council.py selftest
python3 tools/council.py prepare --run northstar-2026-10-28 --round 1 --doc Bennett-NorthStar.md
python3 tools/council.py prepare --run isa-2026-10-28 --round N --doc Bennett-ISA-Spec.md \
        --rubric docs/council/rubric-isa.md --context Bennett-NorthStar.md \
        --subject "..." --precheck "python3 tools/isa_check.py"
python3 tools/isa_check.py        # the pre-flight on its own
python3 tools/council.py run     --run northstar-2026-10-28 --round 1 [--member codex …] [--dry-run]
python3 tools/council.py status  --run northstar-2026-10-28
python3 tools/council.py aggregate --run northstar-2026-10-28 --round 1
```

## Convergence process (adopted 2026-10-28 after ISA rounds 1–4)

The north star converged in six rounds; the ISA spec oscillated (2.12 →
2.83 → 3.04 → 2.83) because each round was a full rewrite that introduced
new mechanical errors, and because members were grading field-level
completeness of contracts as blockers. The process is now:

1. **Surgical edits between rounds, never rewrites.** Accepted text does
   not move. The editor applies each blocker's fix in place and records
   it in `round-<N>/response.md`.
2. **Pre-flight before every round.** `prepare --precheck` runs
   `tools/isa_check.py`: the spec's example objects must parse per the
   grammar, their hashes must reproduce from the spec's own envelope rule,
   their layouts must be disjoint, and their stated results must hold on
   a reference interpreter. A round is not prepared if the check fails.
   Add every new example or claim to the check before grading it.
3. **Diff attached.** `prepare` attaches the unified diff from the previous
   snapshot; members confirm fixes there first, then hunt new S0 issues.
4. **S0 only blocks.** The rubric defines blocking as "two conformant
   implementations would differ, or unsound, or contradictory, or a
   missing normative decision". S1 (implementer must choose, any choice

 succeeded in 99ms:
## 10. Launch ABI (launch 0.9)

**Model.** A run executes a device CC entry; the host passes classical
arguments, register ids and the entropy tape, receives classical
results, fault codes and declared measurements; host services are
reached by `hostcall`. **Positional binding**: argument i of `bennett_
run` binds to parameter i of the entry's signature: `INT(width, bytes)`
to `iN` with width = N (else `E_ARG`), `F64` to `f64`, `QREG` to `qreg[n]`
with the register's n or to `qreg[*]` with any width, `TOKEN` to `token`
(a valid token, §5.4). Arguments and the entropy tape
are **copied** at `bennett_run`; the caller may free its buffers
afterwards. Host-minted tokens are scoped to the program and destroyed
at unload; device-minted tokens to their run (§5.4).
Accessor-returned pointers are valid until the owning object is freed.
`bennett_unload` with a run not CLOSED: `E_INVALID_STATE`.

**Invocation tree.** A run record is a tree (CC segments, R calls, Q
calls); an R call's subtree is owned by its R history; `bennett_reverse`
reverses post-order: CC segments by their run history, R calls by LIFO
reversal of their history (§7.4, reversed segments and `uncall` frames
skipped), top-level Q calls by re-application / adjoint. A fault record
is terminal (§4.9): the run is `FAILED`, and every activation open at
the fault keeps its frames, which reversal undoes like those of a call
that returned.

**States.** `CREATED → RUNNING → (SUSPENDED ⇄ RUNNING) → (HOST_REQUEST ⇄
RUNNING) → COMPLETED | FAILED`; `COMPLETED → REVERSED`; `FAILED →
REVERSED`; any of
`COMPLETED/FAILED/REVERSED → CLOSED` (`bennett_run_close` releases the
record; register ids created by the run remain valid unless measured or
freed). A run owns the registers and memory it touches exclusively while
not CLOSED; a register reached by `qreg.from_id` is touched from then
on; `bennett_qreg_prepare`, `bennett_qreg_free` and a `bennett_run`
argument naming a register owned by another run that is not CLOSED are
`E_INVALID_STATE`. **Reverse eligibility**: `COMPLETED` or `FAILED`; no `measure`
# fd0 — exact import and hierarchical-estimate text for council review

For `b-isa-spec-fd0`, originating at `b-targets-gyp`. Proposal only:
**target/cert 0.10**, with full-version publication under the repository's
change rules. This does not amend 0.9, its catalog, or any consumer.

## Relationship to sol-contract and the defect

Read [the earlier fd0 derivation](../2026-11-02-contract-fd0.md) first.
This note supplies its missing field-level contract, worked serialized
import and acceptance vectors; it does not repeat its alternatives review.
The earlier note correctly identifies that 0.9 §8.3's
`imports.{model}.{version,map}` cannot bind code distance or formulas to
logical costs, and §8.2's leaf-event aggregation cannot express a factory
hierarchy. `bd show b-isa-spec-fd0` confirms the missing exponentiation,
code-distance selection and symbolic-count questions. Both published
versions' §8.1 languages lack the required parameter/quantity bindings.

Differences from the earlier sketch are deliberate:

- The extension is nested under `imports.<name>.model`; the old numeric
  import shape is retained. No new top-level `model_parameters` namespace
  or implicit vendor-name binding is needed.
- Source identity hashes **raw supplied bytes**, with the bytes embedded;
  a second hash identifies the canonical normalized model. Canonicalizing
  a vendor file first could lose significant numeric spelling or metadata.
- Units have dimensions and scales, not just labels. Parameters distinguish
  hardware, code distance and algorithm inputs. A code distance is positive
  integral; its declared domain chooses parity. An unconditional odd-only
  rule would wrongly exclude codes with even distance.
- A new `model` resource kind holds named non-additive outputs. A numeric
  physical/logical ratio is not summed as a qubit count, nor is per-cycle
  risk a gate epsilon. Legacy count charges keep their old meaning.
- Every numeric substitution has a JSON Pointer, unit conversion, rounding
  rule and checked resolved value. The hierarchy has an exact node grammar,
  half-open intervals and single-accounted shared-factory reservations.
- Symbolic models are valid inputs. Unresolved **operational** bindings
  prevent producing a concrete schedule, not loading/executing an otherwise
  valid object. Unsupported source expressions stay losslessly present as
  explicit obligations. That preservation is not a claim of full adapter
  implementation: fd0 remains a blocker until promised adapters work.

North Star §§4.3–4.4, §5.1 and §7's Qualtran/Azure RE row require all named
resources, independent checking, symbolic hierarchy and models as data.
All §8 demo rungs, especially Grover over unmodified SQLite with its
N=2^20 estimate, remain unchanged. A scalar-only subset, source rewrite,
mandatory gate materialization, or rejecting algorithms because a model
is symbolic would narrow that scope. The proposal below retains symbolic
models and a concrete instantiation path without making either executor
understand a vendor's implementation language.

## Publication edits

In the **new version**, extend §8.2's resource kind enumeration with
`"model"`; append `dimension` and `scale` metadata as specified below;
extend §8.3's `imports` entry with optional `model`; add §8.5 below; add
`model_estimates?` to §12.3 region records and the §12.3 paragraph below.
Add a §0 semantic entry referencing fd0/gyp and suite E/H vectors.
The text from “8.5” through “End normative insertion” is the proposed
normative insertion, including its tables. No unspecified field or
inferred default is part of it.

### 8.5 Imported models, parameters and hierarchical estimates (proposed normative text)

An import MAY retain the legacy form `{version:string,map:object}`. Such
an entry has its existing scalar import meaning; no QEC selection,
hierarchy, physical-error guarantee or new parameter is inferred from it.
An entry using this section has exactly `{version,map,model}`. `version`
is the nonempty imported-model version, not the ISA sub-version.
`model.schema` MUST equal `"bennett.import-model/1"`. All fields shown
| d=4 under this model's declared odd domain; d=0 | Domain error. Another model declaring even distance with appropriate formulas remains legal. |
| Null N; N=0; N=3 | Symbolic repeat / empty composition / three copies, respectively. |
| Null hardware parameter used by a binding | Valid symbolic template; incomplete concrete schedule, naming that parameter; unrelated object execution unaffected. |
| Forward quantity reference; quantity/invoke cycle even under repeat 0 | Forward reference valid; either cycle error. |
| Division by zero; negative root; nonintegral pow exponent; zero^zero | Exact expression error. Integer negative powers such as 2^−3=1/8 valid. |
| sqrt(9/4); sqrt(2) | Exact 3/2; exact algebraic expression or verified outward enclosure. At 3 places sqrt(2) is enclosed by [1.414,1.415]. |
| Exact unit conversion factor `"1/3"` | Preserve the rational factor without rounding or a JSON binary64 restriction. |
| 1/3 with upper/lower at 3 places | 0.334/0.333; exact rational mode retains 1/3. |
| Add time and dimensionless risk; bind physical/logical ratio into time | Unit error, no heuristic unit/name conversion. |
| Unknown target path; path into epsilon; duplicate path; bad escaping | Binding error, before any target realization is used. |
| Source byte/hash mismatch; same name with another source version | Mismatch error; distinct identity, respectively. |
| Missing mapped quantity; unaligned quantity with newly declared model resource | Missing output error; valid preserved named resource, respectively. |
| Two quantities mapped to one aligned resource | Preserve both quantity-keyed displays; charge only the explicitly composed counts. |
| Unknown expression field / `var:eps` / accuracy bucket | Invalid normalized expression, never a model epsilon binding. |
| Unsupported source expression at a retained external location | Unchecked report with complete source/obligation; no invented value or algorithm rejection. |
| Three independent sequential/parallel cycles | Same count/occupancy, different makespan and peak as above. |
| Duplicate occurrence of one DAG child | Counts and private demand multiply; DAG storage sharing does not share a physical instance. |
| Shared factory sequence / parallel with one slot | One factory charged once / infeasible simultaneous use; serialized alternative remains legal. |
| Shared interval outside root time or not covering use | Error at reservation/use. Touching intervals have no overlap. |
| Cross-model symbolic invoke of an algorithm argument | Convert dimensions/units, retain caller dependency, qualify identities; no hardware override. |
| Report/certificate with wrong target, context or model hash | Reject the asserted report; no first-context or old-hash cache fallback. |
| Positive physical risk with exact logical gates | Risk output/charge remains nonzero; epsilon and logical fault claims unchanged. |
| Model objective with and without an exact root selector | Bound scalar under ordinary objective rules / unresolved objective, never implicit scalarization. |

The executable check below covers the fixture's scalar arithmetic and
unit conversions; parameter/formula negative cases; forward reference and
zero-multiplied cycle; exact rational roots/powers and rational enclosure
inequalities; sequence/parallel/repeat/shared-accounting examples; hashes;
and document structure. Cross-model invokes, actual operational target
resolution, source adapter translation, generic algebraic evaluation,
context validation and the remaining consumer acceptance vectors remain **proposed**.
A small rational example checker is not an implementation of this format.

## Consumer impact, release and what remains for 0.10

For 0.9 retain concrete scalar target instances when fully bound, their
original model/provenance, and all promised algorithm scope. This is a
compatibility path, not completion of symbolic import support. Do not
pretend a new JSON field is already admitted by 0.9's closed schema. The
reference interpreter's current declared 0.8 surface also does not gain
0.10 admission through a documentation proposal.

For 0.10, target importers parse the pinned source version, preserve raw
bytes, normalize formulas/units/domains, and supply resolved bindings.
The scheduler selects distance using classical data, creates a distinct
resolved target, and evaluates model objectives without materializing all
gates. The estimator retains symbolic hierarchies and reports intervals,
shared factories and named quantities. The VM/circuit executors preserve
the ordinary logical machine semantics and use the resolved costs;
physical simulation is not inferred from a resource-risk output. The
independent checker verifies translation where supported, target/report
identity, arithmetic, accounting and truthful obligation labels. The
conformance harness must run all format vectors with separate producers
and checker; a consumer should not share a vendor importer as its sole
verification oracle across a published contract boundary.

No reference catalog recipe, source syntax or 0.9 envelope changes in
this proposal. Under the normal full-version 0.10 publication, affected
semantic envelopes and source hashes change because their version changes;
do not promise a global no-repin migration. Target hashes change for new
models/resolved distances; target-bound schedules and certificates must
be re-bound and checked. Algorithms' estimate parameters and hierarchy
change the report/certificate bytes, independently of source identity.
Catalog bytes/hash change only if separately revised; fd0 needs no such
recipe change. No artifact is re-pinned by this job.

Remaining 0.10 work is concrete: council acceptance of these new
encodings and unit/accounting rules; machine-readable target/certificate
schemas and vectors; source-format/version adapters including retained
external expressions; an independent translation checker or versioned
checkable witness mechanism for each such expression; scalable symbolic
DAG evaluation, distance-search implementation and a versioned checkable witness for
optimality claims when requested; operational-resolution and context
validation in consumers; compatibility and publication tooling. Routing,
physical pulse/noise simulation and a general proof that vendor QEC models
predict hardware are separate mechanics/assumptions, not hidden obligations
this arithmetic proposal claims to solve. It supplies no automatic
independence law for physical failures and no physical probability to
logical-norm conversion. A user may import an explicit, separately checked
risk-composition formula as model data; no such capability is prohibited.

Keep **b-isa-spec-fd0** open for that contract/adapter work and
**b-targets-gyp** open at its owning repository until the promised complete
path exists. Preserving an unchecked external obligation is honest interim
reporting, not a non-narrowing substitute for implementing it. Neither bead
was mutated by this notes-only job. The exact new mechanism is described
above; no new cross-repo issue is necessary to name the same blocker.

- **Structured `steps`: no longer required.** A blocking item may be `steps` or free-text `problem`; `kind`

- **Sweep before revote.** At the second blocker of one host-resource class, the editor builds the class
  sweep (failure injection across every stage) before the next vote; no acceptance vote is cast on a
  class with a known second instance.

  pass until an implementer (VM, plugin) names the gap it hits; proofs file beads, editors do not act on
  them unprompted.

  the chat and the worklog; `isa-0.9.PROGRESS.md` is cut to its latest block plus a resume list, like the

# Agent Instructions

This project uses **bd** (beads) for issue tracking. Run `bd prime` for full workflow context.

> **Architecture in one line:** Issues live in a local Dolt database
> (`.beads/dolt/`); cross-machine sync uses `bd dolt push/pull` (a
> git-compatible protocol), stored under `refs/dolt/data` on your git
> remote — separate from `refs/heads/*` where your code lives.
> `.beads/issues.jsonl` is a passive export, not the wire protocol.
>
> See [sync-concepts](https://github.com/gastownhall/beads/blob/main/docs/core-concepts/sync-concepts.md)
> for the one-screen overview and anti-patterns (don't treat JSONL as the
> source of truth; don't `bd import` during normal operation; don't
> reach for third-party Dolt hosting before trying the default).

## Quick Reference

```bash
bd ready              # Find available work
bd show <id>          # View issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim work atomically
bd close <id>         # Complete work
bd dolt push          # Push beads data to remote
```

## Non-Interactive Shell Commands

**ALWAYS use non-interactive flags** with file operations to avoid hanging on confirmation prompts.

Shell commands like `cp`, `mv`, and `rm` may be aliased to include `-i` (interactive) mode on some systems, causing the agent to hang indefinitely waiting for y/n input.

**Use these forms instead:**
```bash
# Force overwrite without prompting
cp -f source dest           # NOT: cp source dest
mv -f source dest           # NOT: mv source dest
rm -f file                  # NOT: rm file

# For recursive operations
rm -rf directory            # NOT: rm -r directory
cp -rf source dest          # NOT: cp -r source dest
```

**Other commands that may prompt:**
- `scp` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` for non-interactive
- `ssh` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` to fail instead of prompting
- `apt-get` - use `-y` flag
- `brew` - use `HOMEBREW_NO_AUTO_UPDATE=1` env var

## Beads Issue Tracker

This project uses **bd (beads)** for issue tracking. Run `bd prime` to see full workflow context and commands.

### Quick Reference

```bash
bd ready              # Find available work
bd show <id>          # View issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim work
bd close <id>         # Complete work
```

### Rules

- Use `bd` for ALL task tracking — do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown TODO lists
- Run `bd prime` for detailed command reference and session close protocol
- Use `bd remember` for persistent knowledge — do NOT use MEMORY.md files

**Architecture in one line:** issues live in a local Dolt DB; sync uses `refs/dolt/data` on your git remote; `.beads/issues.jsonl` is a passive export. See https://github.com/gastownhall/beads/blob/main/docs/core-concepts/sync-concepts.md for details and anti-patterns.

## Agent Context Profiles

The managed Beads block is task-tracking guidance, not permission to override repository, user, or orchestrator instructions.

- **Conservative (default)**: Use `bd` for task tracking. Do not run git commits, git pushes, or Dolt remote sync unless explicitly asked. At handoff, report changed files, validation, and suggested next commands.
- **Minimal**: Keep tool instruction files as pointers to `bd prime`; use the same conservative git policy unless active instructions say otherwise.
- **Team-maintainer**: Only when the repository explicitly opts in, agents may close beads, run quality gates, commit, and push as part of session close. A current "do not commit" or "do not push" instruction still wins.

## Session Completion

This protocol applies when ending a Beads implementation workflow. It is subordinate to explicit user, repository, and orchestrator instructions.

1. **File issues for remaining work** - Create beads for anything that needs follow-up
2. **Run quality gates** (if code changed) - Tests, linters, builds
3. **Update issue status** - Close finished work, update in-progress items
4. **Handle git/sync by active profile**:
   ```bash
   # Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval.
   git status

   # Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
   git pull --rebase
   bd dolt push
   git push
   git status
   ```
5. **Hand off** - Summarize changes, validation, issue status, and any blocked sync/commit/push step

**Critical rules:**
- Explicit user or orchestrator instructions override this Beads block.
- Do not commit or push without clear authority from the active profile or the current user request.
- If a required sync or push is blocked, stop and report the exact command and error.

 succeeded in 74ms:
### 8.1 Format and expressions

A target is a JSON document in RFC 8785 canonical form; `target_hash` =
SHA-256 of those bytes; numbers are exact rationals from their decimal
text. Cost expressions are a closed JSON AST:

```
  expr := number | {"var": v} | {"op": o, "args": [expr, …]} | {"table": name, "index": "k" | "eps_decade"}
  v    := "k" | "c" | "eps" | "a" | "w" | "i" | "theta"
  o    := "+" (2) | "-" (2) | "*" (2) | "/" (2) | "max" (2) | "min" (2) | "ceil" (1) | "log2inv_eps" (0)
```

`log2inv_eps` = the exact integer min{ j ≥ 0 : eps · 2^j ≥ 1 } for 0 <
eps ≤ 1; eps outside (0, 1] is a load-time error; an entry with
`epsilon` 0 MUST NOT reference `eps` or `log2inv_eps`. Division by zero
and a table index outside the array are load-time errors. Costs MUST be
nonnegative and finite.

**Variable binding** by evaluation context; referencing a variable that
the context does not bind is a load-time error:

| Context | Bound variables |
|---|---|
| a `gates` entry's `epsilon` and `cost` | `k` = the gate's own control count; `c` = its total control count (`k` + effective control count, §8.3); `eps` = the operation's `epsilon_share` from the schedule (§11) |
| a scheme's `ancillae.count`, template wire subscripts and `repeat` bounds | `k`, `c` as above; `i` inside a `repeat`; `theta` for a parametric scheme |
| a template gate's `params` | `theta`, `i` |
| `qram` costs | `a` = the region's address width A; `w` = its cell width W; `c` |
| a frame descriptor (§9.4) | `c` only |
| `classes.*`, `capabilities.*`, `resources.*.rate` | none (numbers only) |

**Requested versus achieved accuracy.** `eps` is the `epsilon_share`
the schedule assigns to the operation (§11; the default share is the
region's `epsilon` divided by its number of ε > 0 operations). A `gates`
entry whose `epsilon` expression evaluates to a value > `eps` does not
match the operation and matching continues with the next entry; the
error charged to a matched operation is the entry's evaluated `epsilon`;
an entry with `epsilon` 0 is exact and matches every `eps`.

### 11.1 Reference-library contract (K11)

**ISA-REF-LIB** (K11; b-isa-spec-8r4, from bennett-vm-47t).
This is the decided 0.9 contract. The catalog bound as ref version 0.9
is the file `spec/ref-0.9.json` whose four-line envelope hashes to
`5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197`.

accepted catalog snapshot. Consumers lacking the bound library report

documented in `spec/ref-0.9-draft.README.md`. The JSON file is RFC 8785 canonical
UTF-8, with no trailing LF, containing exactly `isa`, `name`, `version`
and `recipes`. The first three are strings `0.9`, `ref`, `0.9`.
`recipes` is an array in bytewise `kind` order, then increasing parameter
tuple order. Each instantiated recipe has `kind`, its integer width or
other named integer parameters, `operations`, `scratch`, `removable`
and `source` (the motivating ISA sentence). A parameterised recipe
uses `parameters` (an ordered array of formals defined below) and `body` instead of the
instance's `operations`; its body uses the closed constructors below.
Recipes cover every §4 instruction/row, the §3.6 step, §3.7 activation
operations and §5.2 construction, with no implementation-private leaf.
Classical instructions/effects remain classical leaf events (§8.2),
not invented quantum gates. The README documents schema, parameters,
coverage and witnesses; it is not hashed and cannot alter operations.

The library hash is SHA-256 of this UTF-8 envelope (every line ends LF,
including the last); the fourth line is the **entire** canonical library
JSON, including all recipes, sources and versions, without an embedded
hash field:

```
isa=0.9
expansion=ref
version=0.9
<JCS library document>
```

`bindings.expansion` names `ref`, version `0.9`, and this 64-lowercase-hex
hash. This adopts §9.2's version-prefixed, LF-terminated **envelope
convention**, with explicit expansion-domain and library-version lines;
it is a new library rule, not §9.2's symbol signature/SCC envelope.
Motivation (0.8 §11): "a versioned document, identified in the schedule's
`bindings.expansion` as { name, version, hash }"; 0.8 §9.2: "Semantic
envelope ... every part ends with LF". A changed recipe, source, scratch
name or ordering changes the library hash; no schedule/certificate can
silently inherit that change (§12.1's realised-subject binding).

**Expansion and ordinals.** Recipe bodies are ordered arrays
of constructors: a gate (`gate`, ordered `wires`, optional `params`), an
## 9. Objects, identity, linking, reversible ABI (object 0.9)

### 9.1 Sections and schemas

Order fixed: `symbols imports functions data devices layout schedule
certificate coverage`; each at most once; unknown sections or fields are
errors. Metadata sections are one JCS line each, an object:

```
  symbols:  { "<local index>": { "name", "kind": "cc"|"r"|"oracle"|"action"|"perm_action"|"lproc"|"data", "signature": <sig>, "interface_hash" } }
  imports:  { "<name>": { "signature": <sig>, "pinned_closure_hash"?: hex } }
  devices:  { "models": { "<dev>": { "version": "0.9" } }, "services": { "<reverse-dns id>": { "version": "…" } } }
  layout:   { "ptr_width", "H", "A_tab", "S_cells", "null_size", "heap_base", "heap_limit_max",
              "globals": { "<name>": { "base", "size", "image": "<data name>" } },
              "interface_regions": { "<oracle>": { "<region>": { "base" } } }, "block_image"?: "<data name>" }
  schedule: §11      certificate: §12      coverage: { "entries": [ §13 ] }
  <sig>:    kind "oracle": { "tier":"q", "kind", "in":[w…], "tgt":w, "fault":F, "mem":[{"A","W","role"}], "args":[types], "abi":"0.9" }
            kind "action"/"perm_action": { "tier":"q", "kind", "mut":[w…], "in":[w…], "mem":[…], "args":[types], "bounds":[…], "abi" }
            kind "lproc": { "tier":"q", "kind", "params":[w…], "result":w, "tokens":["token"…], "args":[types], "mem":[{"A","W","role":"in"}], "abi" }
            kind "cc"/"r": { "tier", "kind", "params":[types], "results":[types], "abi" }
            kind "data": { "tier":"-", "kind", "size", "abi" }
```

Integers that may exceed 2^53 are decimal strings, and a JSON number ≥
2^53 anywhere in metadata is an error. `data` lines are `NAME SP HEX`.
**Encodings.** `types` in `args`, `params` and `results` are the §16.2
type spellings as JSON strings (`"i64"`, `"ptr"`, `"f64"`, `"token"`,
`"qreg[8]"`); `w` in `in`, `tgt`, `mut`, lproc `params` and `result` is
the integer bundle or location width (§16.3). An lproc's `tokens`,
`args` and `mem` arrays are mandatory (empty arrays when absent from the
header); `args` excludes tokens and `mem` has only `in` roles (§3.7).
Array order is header order within each group. A **program-point id**
(§9.2) is the JSON array `[interface_hash, block name, ordinal]`.
Certificate `frame` fields use exactly the typed-record schema of §9.4;
unknown fields are errors there as in every metadata record.
`devices.models` lists every §7.3 model that an `effect` of the object
uses (an `effect` on an unlisted §7.3 model is a load error; a device
outside §7.3 is never listed and faults at run time, §17 item 3; a key
that is not a §7.3 model is an unknown field); `devices.services` lists
every `hostcall` service (§10). An interface region's name (its param
name and its `layout.interface_regions.<oracle>` key) MUST differ from
every symbol and import name of the object, since `@name` in `lift.addr`
denotes either (§9.2).

### 9.2 Identity
entries make the schedule invalid** (document order is never
significant). Selectors are resolved independently per `kind`. Defaults
for uncovered operations: `reverse`; `native` if the gate matches a
`gates` entry, else the first matching scheme; class = the first class
declared in the target's `classes` object (JCS order) that has capacity
for it; sequential timing in generated order. **Timing**: a `start` time
MUST be ≥ the end of every operation the operation depends on (data,
wire lifetime, region cell version, effect order); an entry violating
this makes the schedule invalid. **Obligations**: satisfy §6; respect
capacity; report every resource; be reproducible from the request plus
scheduler version. A consumer validates every binding and every path
against the object and executes verbatim or rejects (`E_SCHEDULE_
INFEASIBLE`).

## 1. Scope and conformance

### 1.1 What the ISA is

A program model for classical, reversible and coherent computation with
one semantics, executed by the VM (`bennett-vm`) and the circuit
specialiser (`bennett-circuits`), produced by the plugin
(`bennett-plugin`) through the dialect's reference semantics, scheduled
through §11, described for hardware by target descriptions (§8). Nothing
here refers to LLVM.

### 1.2 Conformance classes

| Class | Who | Obligation |
|---|---|---|
| Executor | VM, specialiser, hardware stacks | §3–§7, §14 exactly, and the checking of inverse-pair claims (§4.5, §12.1; a certificate applies when its `closure_hash` binding equals the region's); suites A–E, H |
| Producer | plugin, library compilers | well-formed objects (§9, §16) and regions (§5, §6); suite F |
| Scheduler | `bennett-sched` and competitors | §11; only schedules valid under §6; suite H |
| Checker | `bennett-conformance` | validates certificates (§12) without trusting producers |
| Target author | vendors | target descriptions (§8); suite E |

### 1.3 Deferred *(asserted non-semantic)*

Binary encodings (§16 text is the interchange form); mechanisation of
§14; device models beyond §7.3; the gate-name registry process; the
memory layout of the opaque C objects of §10–§11 (every constructor,
accessor and destructor is listed). **Minimality claims** (north star
§5) are not supported by cert 0.9 and MUST NOT appear in a certificate.
Decisions deferred to implementation, each with its default, are listed
in §17.

## 2. Terminology

- **Tier.** CC, R, Q. **Wire**, **bundle** `wire[N]` (little-endian).
- **Location** `reg[N]`: a named mutable cell of an oracle or lproc
  register file. **Pseudo-location**: an oracle interface input or
  classical argument, or an lproc classical parameter (§3.7), readable
  only in its declaring function, never written.
- **Region**: a Q function with a declared interface: **lifted oracle**
  (§5.2) or **quantum action** (§5.3); **permutation action** (§5.3).
- **lproc**: a lifted procedure with its own params and locations; it
  resolves names only to its own params and locations (§3.7).
- **Budget**: classical (B, heap_limit, stack_limit, depth_limit).
- **Frame**: per machine step: `old_pc`, slots (assignment, capture and
  delivery slots, §3.6), `flag`, `succ`.
- **Basis configuration**: classical values on every component of D.
- **Gate classes.** Permutation: `x cx ccx mcx swap`. Diagonal: `s sdg t
  tdg cz rz`. Non-diagonal: `h rx ry`.
- **Verifying executor**: an executor that can decide exactly whether a
  set of wires is clean on every basis configuration (a state-vector or
  branch-enumeration simulator); every other executor (hardware) is
  **non-verifying**. Rules that depend on the difference say which
  applies (§3.3 `qreg.free`, §10, §17 item 14).
- **Effective control context** at a point: the set of wires on the
  active predicate stack plus the inherited control context (the control
  wires of every enclosing `ctl` and predicate stack of every enclosing
  invocation, transitively); duplicates count once. Its size is the
  **effective control count**.

### 8.5 Imported models, parameters and hierarchical estimates (proposed normative text)

An import MAY retain the legacy form `{version:string,map:object}`. Such
an entry has its existing scalar import meaning; no QEC selection,
hierarchy, physical-error guarantee or new parameter is inferred from it.
An entry using this section has exactly `{version,map,model}`. `version`
is the nonempty imported-model version, not the ISA sub-version.
`model.schema` MUST equal `"bennett.import-model/1"`. All fields shown
below are required unless suffixed `?`; absent optional arrays/maps are
empty. Duplicate JSON keys, unknown fields, and unknown schema versions
are invalid model encodings, rather than instructions to ignore data.

**Scalar and unit types.** In the following schemas `S` is a nonempty
Unicode string compared bytewise, `H` is exactly 64 lowercase hexadecimal
digits, `Hex` is an even-length lowercase hexadecimal string (possibly
empty), and `Q` is a reduced rational encoded as a string: `0` or a
canonical signed decimal numerator, optionally followed by `/` and a
positive canonical denominator greater than one. No leading `+`, leading
zero, `-0`, denominator one, or reducible fraction is permitted. `Z` is
the integral subset of Q, `N` the nonnegative integers, and `P` the strictly
positive rationals. Mathematical integer ranges are unbounded; an implementation
resource limit is an explicit unfinished-operation diagnostic, not a
model-domain restriction or an executed program fault.

A `Unit` is `{dimension:{S:Q},scale:P}`. Its dimension map omits zero
exponents. Axis `s` denotes seconds; `physical_qubit` and `logical_qubit`
are distinct axes. Other axes MUST include their source format and version in the axis
identifier, unless an explicit dimension-preserving conversion is present in the normalized
formula. An axis spelling itself is its identity; there is no implicit
unit-equivalence registry.
Equal labels alone imply no equivalence. A numeric value x in this unit
has base value x*scale and the given dimension vector. Fractional
exponents are admitted. Dimensionless units have `{}`. Unit metadata is
MUST be ≥ the end of every operation the operation depends on (data,
wire lifetime, region cell version, effect order); an entry violating
this makes the schedule invalid. **Obligations**: satisfy §6; respect
capacity; report every resource; be reproducible from the request plus
scheduler version.

**Validation stages (K55).** A consumer validates every binding and
every path against the object, including the maximal-match condition
at the stages specified here, and executes verbatim or rejects with
`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE`. [deps: ISA-9.2, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S2, ISA-11-P6.S3, ISA-11-P6.S4, ISA-11-P7.S2, ISA-11-P7.S3, ISA-11-P7.S7]
For admission, a nested invocation budget is **object-derivable** exactly
when substituting literal integer constants and signature-positional
classical/token forwarding into the four operands of `budget.mint`
yields one tuple for that call site; an unresolved root parameter,
conflicting tuples or any other unresolved operand makes it
non-derivable. [deps: ISA-4.1, ISA-3.7, ISA-5.4, ISA-10]
